Frequently asked questions

How does Google protect my data?

The addition of these AI features includes the same robust data protection and security standards that come with all Google Workspace services:

  • Users get enterprise-grade data protections when they use the Gemini app. Submissions aren’t reviewed by humans or otherwise used to train models.
  • Your interactions with Gemini in Google Workspace apps stay within your organization. Prompts and content you generate with these features is stored alongside your Workspace content and is never shared outside your organization.
  • Your existing Google Workspace protections are automatically applied. These AI features bring the same enterprise-grade security as the rest of Google Workspace, automatically applying your organization’s existing controls and data handling practices, such as data-regions policies and Data Loss Prevention.
  • Your content is not used for any other customers. None of your content is used for model training outside of your domain without permission. Learn how Google Workspace keeps your data secure and prioritizes privacy.

If you have a Google AI plan, learn how Gemini protects your data.

Can I control access to these AI features?

You have the option to turn off some AI features. Learn how to turn Google Workspace smart features on or off.

In addition, if you have a work or school account, your administrator can also:

  • Turn off access to the Gemini App.
  • Turn off Gemini Notebook as an additional service.
  • Stop users from letting Google AI take notes in meetings. Your existing Google Workspace data protections are automatically applied to AI features.

If you have a Google AI plan, learn how Gemini protects your data.

Are there tips for using Gemini effectively?

You can help improve your success with Gemini with these basic best practices:

  • Learn how to write good prompts for Gemini.
  • Use this cheat sheet for an overview of Gemini features.

Improve your content with Gemini in Google Slides

Important: This feature requires an eligible Google Workspace or Google AI plan. Learn about Gemini features and plans.

You can use text refining suggestions with Gemini in Google Slides to improve the quality of your content.

Use “Refine” to:

  • Shorten text
  • Rephrase sentences
  • Make the tone of voice more formal
  • Convert paragraphs into bullet points This feature is currently available on desktop.

Refine your text

  • On your computer, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Click on a text box Refine .
  • Type a custom prompt or choose a refinement option:
    • Shorten
    • Rephrase
    • More formal
    • Bulletize
  • Optional: To generate another version:
    • You can try a custom prompt.
    • Or you can click Refine and choose a refinement option.
  • To replace existing content with the generated text, click Replace.
  • Optional: To insert the generated text below the existing content, click the Down arrow Insert.

Give feedback about “Refine writing” in Slides

Google Workspace with Gemini is constantly learning and may not be able to support your request.

If you get a suggestion that’s inaccurate or that you feel is unsafe, you can let us know by submitting feedback. Your feedback can help improve AI-assisted Workspace features and broader Google efforts in AI.

Because feedback may be human readable, do not submit data that contains personal, confidential, or sensitive information.

  • Below the generated output, select Good suggestion or Bad suggestion .
  • If you select Bad suggestion you can enter additional feedback and select the issue you found.
  • Click Submit. To provide general feedback on this feature, go to Help Help Slides improve.

To report a legal issue, create a request.

Learn about Gemini feature suggestions

  • Gemini feature suggestions don’t represent Google’s views, and should not be attributed to Google.
  • Don’t rely on Gemini features as medical, legal, financial or other professional advice.
  • Gemini features may suggest inaccurate or inappropriate information. Your feedback makes Gemini more helpful and safe.
  • Enterprise end users can submit feedback about their experience using this feature. End users are informed before they submit feedback that the feedback data shouldn’t contain personal, sensitive, or confidential information.

Learn about AI labels & content credentials

On images, videos, and audio generated by Gemini in supported Workspace apps, you will see “Content Credentials”, a technology that provides media history and transparency about the use of artificial intelligence (AI).

Understand AI labels & content credentials

Important: If the app is listed as “Google Media Processing Services”, it means the file was definitely made or changed using AI. This is the official digital signature for content produced by Google’s generative models.

Hidden digital labels are inside supported files, like images, videos, and audio. They give you details about:

  • Provider: The organization that created or published the content (e.g., Google).

  • App: The specific software or tool used to generate or modify the content. Content credentials can:

  • Provide details about the origin and history of a photo or video through a standard that works across devices and apps from different companies.

  • Provide information from the app or device maker about AI or non-AI tools used to create or edit a photo or video. Content Credentials cannot tell you if a photo or video is real or fake, but you can use the information together with the content of the photo or video to make informed decisions.

Inspect credentials within supported editors

For files opened within the Workspace apps listed below, you might find more specific content credential details. Use the entry point for your editor to view credentials:

  • Docs, Slides, Vids (Web): Right-click/context menu Content credentials sidebar.
  • Sheets (Web): Click the 3-dot menu (floating images) or context menu below “Put image over cells” (embedded images) Content credentials side panel.
  • Google Pics: Main menu Content credentials.
  • Drive Audio Overviews: open File Details to show the Details side panel.

Understand technical limitations

While content credentials offer insights into an asset’s history, they operate within specific technical boundaries.

Platform & encryption limitations
  • Web-only inspection: Content credentials inspection is only available when using Google Workspace on a computer web browser.
  • No client-side encryption (CSE) support: Client-side encrypted files do not support C2PA signing.
  • No offline support: Offline files do not support C2PA signing.
Recognize fragile file history

C2PA metadata can be lost or altered. The “chain of custody” may be broken if:

  • You take a screenshot: A new file is created without the original credentials.
  • You resize or re-save: Using standard image or video editing tools can strip embedded metadata.
  • You use third-party tools: Sharing content via certain social media platforms often removes or modifies original metadata.
  • You download or export the file. Tip: SynthID metadata is preserved during most edits, downloads, and exports.

Learn the difference between content credential history and facts

A valid content credential confirms how a digital file was made or edited. It does not confirm the content’s truth or reality.

For instance, an AI tool might generate a realistic image of an event that never happened. While the content credentials will confirm the image was created by a specific AI tool, they do not verify the truthfulness of the image’s subject matter.

Related resources

  • Create Content, Images & Audio Overviews with Gemini in Drive.
  • Learn how Gemini in Gmail, Calendar, Chat, Docs, Drive, Sheets, Slides, Meet & Vids protects your data

Learn how Gemini in Gmail, Calendar, Chat, Docs, Drive, Sheets, Slides, Meet, Vids & Pics protects your data

Across Google and Workspace, we’ve long shared robust privacy commitments that outline how we protect user data and prioritize privacy. Generative AI doesn’t change these commitments — it actually reaffirms their importance.

Our privacy commitments to all Workspace users

Generative AI does not change our foundational privacy protections for giving users choice and control over their data. To that end, here are key facts about how Workspace data is handled.

  • Your data is your data. The content that you put into Google Workspace services (emails, documents, etc.) is yours. We never sell your data, and you can delete your content or export it.
  • Your data stays in Workspace by default. We do not use your Workspace data to train or improve the underlying generative AI and large language models that power Gemini, Search, and other systems outside of Workspace without permission.
  • Your privacy is protected. Interactions with intelligent Workspace features, such as accepting or rejecting spelling suggestions, or reporting spam, are anonymized and/or aggregated and may be used to improve or develop helpful Workspace features like spam protection, spell check, and autocomplete. These Workspace features are developed with strict privacy protections that keep users in control.
  • Your content is not used for ads targeting in Google Workspace. Google Workspace does not collect, scan, or use your content for advertising purposes. Important: If you’re a Workspace user with a personal account and you choose to share data, including your Workspace data, with Gemini Apps or Search services for Personal Intelligence, or through screen actions (including screenshots), this data will be processed according to their respective terms and policies, and may be used for model training and improvement. Learn more in the Gemini Apps Privacy Hub or the Search services Frequently Asked Questions.

These are the privacy commitments that Workspace extends to all of its users. But they aren’t just words. To ensure we continually meet these high standards, independent auditors validate our practices against international standards and best practices.

Specific commitments for Gemini in Gmail, Calendar, Chat, Docs, Drive, Slides, Sheets, Meet, Vids & Pics

For users with Google AI plans, when using Gemini in Gmail, Calendar, Chat, Docs, Drive, Slides, Sheets, Meet, Vids, and Pics, your data is yours and under your control. Whether you ask Gemini to summarize an email or document, write a blog post, or add a unique image to your slide:

  • Gemini in Gmail, Calendar, Chat, Docs, Drive, Slides, Sheets, Meet, Vids, and Pics uses your content in Google Workspace to provide more useful responses to your prompts and doesn’t use your content to train or improve Gemini or other generative AI models.
  • Gemini in Gmail, Calendar, Chat, Docs, Drive, Slides, Sheets, Meet, Vids, and Pics doesn’t store your prompt or the generated output without your permission. Note: For users enrolled in the Google Workspace Experiments program, use of Gemini in Gmail, Calendar, Chat, Docs, Drive, Slides, Sheets, Meet, Vids, and Pics is covered by the Google Workspace Experiments Privacy Notice & Terms for Personal Accounts.

Learn how to use sources with Google Workspace with Gemini

When you use Gemini in Workspace apps like Docs and Drive, you use sources to tell Gemini exactly which information to use in its response.

Sources are files, documents, or contextual info that Gemini uses to provide more relevant and focused answers to your prompts.

With sources, Gemini can:

  • Find information: Get tailored responses or ask for data points found in your files.
  • Summarize documents: Get a summary or extract key points from a file you have as a source.
  • Compare facts between files: Use multiple source files to compare data, insights, or findings across them.

Understand your sources

When you interact with Gemini, you can manage the content that it can access to inform its response. You can:

  • Add a source from Drive
  • Manage source settings
  • Add a missing source
  • Use cell ranges as a source in Sheets
  • Check sources that contribute to Gemini’s response

Add a source from Drive

You can add specific files in Drive when you want Gemini to prioritize these files before it consults other options. When you add a source, it remains active for your entire conversation, providing consistent context for Gemini’s responses. You can add:

  • Google Docs

  • Google Sheets

  • Google Slides

  • PDFs Add a source in Docs, Gmail, or Slides

  • Go to:

    • Docs: docs.google.com
    • Gmail: mail.google.com
    • Slides: slides.google.com

  • At the top right corner, click Ask Gemini .

  • At the bottom of the panel, click Add sources .

  • Click Add from Drive.

  • Select a file.

  • Click Add. Add a source in Drive

  • Go to drive.google.com.

  • At the top right, click Ask Gemini .

  • In the left panel, under “Your sources” or “Project sources,” click Add.

  • Select the files, folders, or emails you want to add as sources.

  • Click Add. Learn how to manage your sources and search settings in Drive.

Add a source in Sheets

  • Go to sheets.google.com.

  • At the top right, click Ask Gemini .

  • At the bottom of the panel, click Sheet .

    • If your sheet is not empty, click Sources .

  • Click Add from Drive.

  • Select a file.

  • Click Add. Tips:

  • To add a file, you need permission to access it. You can only use files you have permission to view in Drive as sources. If you lack file access, you get this alert: “You need access to some files.” Learn how to get permission to open a file or folder.

  • Before you can add a PDF as a source, upload it to Drive. Learn how to upload files and folders to Google Drive.

Manage source settings

Important:

  • Gemini only searches the sources you have selected, but may use any app data enabled by an administrator as well as activity from across Workspace apps to prioritize the most relevant information from available sources. Some sources are on by default, and you can disable them in your source settings at any time.

  • Gemini can reference sources from a previous turn in your current conversation. To exclude a source you had previously included, start a new conversation and go to your source settings to select the sources you want. Manage source settings in Docs, Gmail, or Slides

  • Go to:

    • Docs: docs.google.com
    • Gmail: mail.google.com
    • Slides: slides.google.com

  • At the top right corner, click Ask Gemini .

    • In Docs, you can also add sources from the bottom bar.

  • Click Add sources .

  • Click Gemini search settings.

  • Turn specific search locations on or off:

    • Drive search
    • Gmail search
    • Chat search
    • Web search Manage source settings in Drive

Important: When you start a conversation from scratch, search settings for Drive, Gmail, Chat, and Calendar are turned on by default. Gemini uses these default search settings to answer questions. Web search remains off by default. Learn how to manage your sources and search settings in Drive.

  • Go to drive.google.com.
  • At the top right corner, click Ask Gemini .
  • In the left panel, toggle on Let Gemini search for sources. This enables Gemini to pull broader context from across Google Workspace.
  • If you want to limit locations, leave the primary toggle on but uncheck specific locations:
    • Select Drive to search your wider Drive content beyond pinned sources.
    • Select Chat, Calendar, or Gmail to search recent communications and meeting schedules.
    • Select Web to search the internet for supplemental public data. Manage source settings in Sheets
  • Go to sheets.google.com.
  • At the top right, click Ask Gemini .
  • At the bottom of the panel, click Sheet .
    • If your sheet is not empty, click Sources .
  • Click Gemini search settings.
  • Turn specific search locations on or off:
    • Drive search
    • Gmail search
    • Chat search
    • Web search Manage source settings for skills in Workspace Studio

Important: When you build a skill, search settings for Drive and Web are turned on by default. Gemini uses these default search settings to build the skill.

  • Go to Google Workspace Studio.
  • On the left, select Skills.
  • At the top right, click Create.
  • At the bottom of the skill builder, click Add files & more .
  • Select Gemini search settings.
  • Under “Where Gemini can search,” check or uncheck the sources you want Gemini to access:
    • Drive
    • Gmail
    • Chat
    • Calendar
    • Web

Add a missing source

If a prompt requires a source you haven’t selected in your Gemini search settings, you’ll get a message: “To complete this request, Gemini needs access to [service].”

To fix this issue:

  • In the Gemini in Workspace side panel, click Add Gemini search settings.
  • Under “Where Gemini can search,” check the box next to the missing source.

Use cell ranges as a source in Sheets

In Sheets, if you want Gemini to use a specific range or table in the file instead of the entire sheet:

  • Highlight the cell range in your sheet.

  • Ask Gemini a question that references the range. Tips:

  • Cell ranges are only for the sheet tab you are currently on.

  • Gemini in Sheets might automatically select existing sources, such as other Sheet tabs. If you want to ask a question only about your selected cell range, make sure to deselect the other sources first.

Check sources that contribute to Gemini’s response

After Gemini generates a response, you can review a list of the files that contributed to its answer. Below the response from Gemini, select Sources to find the list of sources.

For all requests that you make to Gemini, there’s a limited processing capacity, often referred to as a context window. If you have too many sources, or include sources with too much text, they may exceed this capacity. In these cases, Gemini might base its response on some, but not all, of the content.

Even when Gemini shows sources, it can still get things wrong. For example:

  • Gemini might miss a source it used, or it may cite a document that is not directly used for a specific piece of information.
  • In some cases, Gemini can make up a source, presenting it as factual even if it wasn’t part of the request. Make sure to double-check the response and verify information with the listed sources, and with other sources you trust, before you rely on the generated content.

Remove image backgrounds in Google Drawings, Slides & Vids

Feature availability:

  • This feature requires an eligible Google Workspace or Google AI plan. Learn how to get started with Google Workspace with Gemini.
  • This feature is also available through our trusted tester program, Google Workspace Experiments.
  • Learn about supported languages for Google Workspace with Gemini. For any image in Google Drawings, Slides, or Vids, you can remove the background.

Remove an image’s background

Important: Vids generative AI features, like “help me create” and image background removal, are only available in English at this time

  • Click an image in a drawing in Google Drawings, a slide in Google Slides, or a scene’s canvas in Google Vids.
  • Click Edit image Remove background.
    • You can also remove the background from the Format menu: At the top, click Format Image Edit image Remove background. Tip: Images with removed backgrounds can’t be reset with Reset image. To go back to the original photo, click Undo or go to “Version history.”

Give feedback on image background removal

Google Workspace with Gemini is constantly learning and may not be able to support your request.

Do not submit data that contains personal, confidential, or sensitive information because feedback can be read by humans.

To give feedback:

  • In the bottom left corner, click Good suggestion or Bad suggestion .
    • If you select Bad suggestion , select the issue you found and enter additional feedback.
  • Select Submit.

Use Deep Research in Gemini in Workspace apps

Important: You can also use Deep Research in Gemini Apps, though certain features may vary from Gemini in Workspace apps. Learn how to use Deep Research in Gemini Apps.

You can conduct in-depth and real-time research on most subjects with Deep Research in Gemini in Workspace. You can change or add other sources, like your personal Gmail or Drive, for your research.

You can use Deep Research for Gemini in:

  • Chat
  • Docs
  • Drive
  • Gmail
  • Sheets
  • Slides

What you need

  • For personal Google Accounts:
    • You need an eligible Google AI plan:
      • Google AI Plus
      • Google AI Pro
      • Google AI Ultra
    • You can also access Deep Research if your account is enrolled in Google Workspace Experiments, which is a trusted tester program to try new AI features. Learn how to get started with Google Workspace Experiments.
  • For work or school accounts: You need an eligible Google Workspace plan. Learn more about Gemini features and plans.

Start a Deep Research report

Start a Deep Research report in Chat

  • In Google Chat, open Ask Gemini .

    • Tip: Learn where to find Ask Gemini in Google Chat.

  • At the bottom, in the text box, enter a question or prompt that explains what you want to research. For example:

    • “Use Deep Research to examine current renewable energy trends and compare them with my project drafts in Drive.”
    • “Use Deep Research to recommend restaurants, attractions, and activities for my upcoming 7-day trip to Oslo, Norway.”

  • Click Send message . Start a Deep Research report in Docs, Drive, Gmail, Sheets, or Slides

  • On your computer, open a Google Workspace app, like Gmail or Docs.

  • On the right, click Ask Gemini .

  • At the bottom, click Tools Deep Research.

  • In the text box, enter a question or prompt that explains what you want to research. For example:

    • “Research current renewable energy trends and compare them with my project drafts in Drive.”
    • “Find recommended restaurants, attractions, and activities for my upcoming 7-day trip to Oslo, Norway.”

  • Click Submit .

Set up the Deep Report report

Step 1: Add sources & review your research plan

After you submit your prompt, you can choose sources to help inform your research.

  • In the sources card, select one or more options:

    • Drive: Analyze files and folders in your Google Drive.
    • Gmail: Include info from your emails.
    • Chat: Include info from your chat messages.
    • Web: Search the public web for the latest info.

  • Optional for Docs, Drive, Gmail, Sheets, and Slides: To choose specific sources for your research, click Add sources and select files from your Drive.

  • Click Next.

    • Gemini creates a research plan for your topic.
    • To update the research plan before you create a report, click Customize.

  • Click Start research. Step 2: Track your research

  • While you work, Gemini researches your topic based on the plan.

  • It can take some time to generate your report since Gemini analyzes many sources.

  • You can close Gemini, start a new chat, or switch to a different Workspace document. The research task continues to run in the background.

  • When the research is complete:

    • Gemini generates the report in a Google Doc.
    • You get an email about your plan’s completion. Step 3: Open your report

  • In your conversation with Gemini, click Open Doc.

    • A new Google Doc opens in a new tab.
    • It includes the findings, citations, and an organized summary of the data found.
    • The report automatically saves to your Drive.

  • In the document, ask Gemini follow-up questions about the report.

    • You can also ask Gemini to refine specific sections.

Find your Deep Research reports

Find your Deep Research reports in Chat

  • In Google Chat, open Ask Gemini .
    • Tip: Learn where to find Ask Gemini in Google Chat.
  • Under “Recent,” select your research conversation.
    • To find more conversations, click Show more. Find your Deep Research reports in Docs, Drive, Gmail, Sheets, or Slides
  • On your computer, open a Google Workspace app, like Gmail or Docs.
  • At the top right, click Ask Gemini .
  • At the top left, click More options .
  • Under “History,” find your research conversation.
    • To find more conversations, click View all. Tips:
  • Your conversations are organized by date, with the most recent at the top.
  • You can also find your research reports at drive.google.com.

Related resources

  • Get started with Google Workspace with Gemini
  • Learn how Gemini in Gmail, Calendar, Chat, Docs, Drive, Sheets, Slides, Meet & Vids protects your data

Use integrations with Gemini in Workspace

With Gemini in Google Workspace, you can connect to and retrieve information from third-party services like Asana and Mailchimp. These are known as integrations.

You can use an integration with Gemini in:

  • Chat

  • Docs

  • Drive

  • Gmail

  • Sheets

  • Slides Important: For specific steps on connecting integrations in Sheets or Chat:

  • Learn more about integrations in Chat

  • Learn more about integrations in Sheets For all other Workspace apps, learn how to use an integration.

What you need

  • A supported personal, work, or school account:
    • For personal accounts, you need:
      • Google AI Pro
      • Google AI Ultra
    • For work or school accounts, you need:
      • Business Standard
      • Business Plus
      • Enterprise Standard
      • Enterprise Plus
      • Enterprise Essentials
      • Enterprise Essential Plus
      • Google AI Pro for Education
  • A required third-party subscription: In addition to any required Google AI plans or Google Workspace editions, some integrations require a subscription to use with Gemini in Workspace. Check the page for the integration when you install it for details.
  • Administrator permission for work or school accounts: Your account’s Google Workspace administrator enables the ability to connect integrations to Gemini in Workspace. If you can’t use an integration, ask your administrator to allow it or connect it for you.

Connect an integration

  • On your computer, go to the Google Workspace Marketplace integrations page.
    • This page provides a pre-filtered view of integrations that work with Gemini in Workspace.
  • Select the integration you want to connect to Gemini in Workspace.
  • Click Connect.
  • To grant the integration access to your Google Account data and complete the connection, follow the on-screen instructions.
    • A third-party helper app might be required. To check if a helper app is needed, review the app’s details page in the Workspace Marketplace. Any requirements are listed in the “Overview” section.

Explore available integrations

Important: Integrations currently only support read actions, such as searching for or finding information. You can’t use an integration to make changes to third-party apps.

Integration

Description

Asana

Find specific projects, tasks, or subtasks within your workspace. You can also check the current status of assigned work.

Atlassian-Rovo (Jira, Confluence)

Track down specific Jira issues and project details. Locate relevant documentation across your Confluence pages.

HubSpot

Access your CRM data to find specific contacts or companies. View the details of active deals directly in your prompt.

Mailchimp

Retrieve details about your email campaigns and audience lists. Pull up recent subscriber reports to review performance.

Monday.com

Query your boards for updates on ongoing projects. Quickly locate individual tasks and their details.

QuickBooks

Retrieve financial records like customer invoices and logged expenses. Look up specific billing details directly from Workspace.

Salesforce

Look up customer contacts and associated companies. Review active leads and pipeline opportunities.

Use an integration

  • On your computer, open a supported Gemini in Workspace app.

  • In your Workspace app, find and open Ask Gemini .

  • In the text box at the bottom, enter @.

  • Select the integration you want to use.

  • Enter your prompt with instructions or questions for Gemini.

  • Press Enter. Examples:

  • “What @Asana issues assigned to me are due today?”

  • “What’s the current pipeline looking like for my open opportunities in @Salesforce right now?”

Delete a connection to an integration

Through your Google Account security settings, you can review and delete connections to third-party integration. When you delete a connection, data sharing between Gemini in Workspace and that integration stops.

To delete an integration:

  • On your computer, open a browser.

  • In your browser, go to Google Account security settings.

  • Find the “Your connections to third-party app & services” card.

  • Click See all connections.

  • Select the app name you want to disconnect.

  • Click Delete all connections you have with [name of app].

  • In the pop-up window, click Confirm. Tip: To manage administrator controls:

  • Learn more about workplace integration steps.

  • Learn how to manage the Marketplace app allowlist.

Get help with Google Docs Editors

How your Google storage works

Each Google Account includes up to 15 GB of storage, which is shared across Gmail, Google Drive, and Google Photos. To add to your storage quota, you can purchase a Google One membership, where available. Occasionally, you may receive more storage from a special promotion or related purchase. Learn how to clean up space or upgrade and how you use your storage.

If you’re ever locked out of your Google Account, recovery options can help you get back in. Learn more about account recovery.

Note: This article is about your Google Account storage. If you use Drive for desktop and your computer shows a different amount of space, learn why your computer’s storage differs from your Google storage.

What affects your Google Account storage

What counts towards your storage quota
  • Original quality photos and videos backed up to Google Photos.
  • High quality (now named Storage saver) and Express quality photos and videos backed up to Google Photos after June 1, 2021. Any photos or videos you’ve backed up in High quality or Express quality before June 1, 2021 will not count toward your Google Account storage. Learn more about this change.
    • Note: Older, inactive photo libraries still occupy active space if they were backed up in Original quality.
  • Gmail messages and attachments, which includes your Spam and Trash folders.
  • Files in Google Drive, which includes PDFs, images, and videos. Files in shared folders only consume the cloud storage quota of the original file owner, never the recipient.
  • Meet call recordings.
  • Files created or edited in collaborative content creation apps like Google Docs, Sheets, Slides, Drawings, Forms, Recorder, Vids, and Pics.
    • Files created or edited after June 1, 2021 count against your quota.
    • Files uploaded or last edited before June 1, 2021 don’t count against your quota.
    • WhatsApp backups on Android.
  • All data managed through the Android backup settings.
    • SMS and MMS messages
    • Call logs and history
    • Device settings
    • App data

What happens when you’re over quota

When you’re over quota, it means you use more storage space than you have available. If you go over your storage quota:

  • You can still sign into and access your Google Account.
  • Most actions on your Drive files will be restricted other than download, delete, and Takeout.
  • You can’t back up any photos and videos to Google Photos.
  • Your ability to send and receive email in Gmail can be impacted.
  • You can’t create new files in collaborative content creation apps like Google Docs, Sheets, Slides, Drawings, Forms, Vids, and Pics. Until you reduce your storage usage, nobody can edit or copy your affected files.
  • You can’t back up new Recorder files.
If you’re over your quota for 2 years or longer

If you don’t free up or purchase more space to get back under quota, all of your content in your Google Account may be removed including in Gmail, Google Photos, Google Drive (this includes Google Docs, Sheets, Slides, Drawings, Forms, Vids, and Pics) and your Android device’s backup.

Before your content is removed, we:

  • Give you notice using email or notifications within the Google products. We will contact you at least 3 months before content is eligible for deletion.
  • Give you the opportunity to avoid deletion (by paying for additional storage or removing files)
  • Give you the opportunity to download your content from our services. Learn how to download your Google data.

How to go back under quota

We provide access to storage management tools that help you identify ways to free up storage space. Another option to free up space is to download your files to your personal device and then delete them from your cloud storage.

After you delete a large number of files, it normally takes up to 48–72 hours for Google’s systems to accurately update the storage space.

If you want more storage space for Gmail, Drive, and Photos, you can upgrade to a larger storage plan with Google One.

Learn more about how to free up storage space.

Learn more about Google storage

How these policies apply to Google Workspace

Some changes regarding quota will apply to some Google Workspace, G Suite for Education and G Suite for Nonprofits plans. Please refer to our Google Workspace Updates post to understand how these changes may impact them.

How these policies apply to Google Sites, Google Keep & other apps

The over quota policy doesn’t apply to:

  • Google Sites
  • Google Keep
  • Blogger
  • YouTube The inactivity policy applies to all content in your Google Account and the account itself.

How these policies apply to supervised child accounts

Supervised accounts can access the Google One storage manager. They can navigate to the Google One storage manager page for Photos, Gmail and Drive on the web.

However, supervised child accounts can’t purchase Google One memberships.

How long until content gets deleted if you’re over quota

If your account is subject to this policy change, we’ll attempt to give you ample notice (at least three months) before your content may be deleted. Your content will be eligible for deletion when you have been over your storage quota for 2 years. To make sure your content is not deleted, either reduce how much storage you’re using or upgrade to a larger storage plan with Google One.

How much notice do you receive before content is deleted

If your account is subject to this policy change, we’ll give you at least 3 months notice before your content may be deleted.

  • We’ll try to reach you through your email address and notifications.
  • Manage your Google Account email. How to preserve content from a deceased loved one

We recognize that many people pass away without leaving clear instructions about how to manage their online accounts. Google can work with immediate family members and representatives (in certain circumstances) to provide content from a deceased user’s account. Learn more about our process for requesting a deceased user’s data.

To let us know in advance what to do with your data in the event of death or prolonged inactivity, learn more about Inactive Account Manager.

Tip: The Inactive Account Manager settings don’t override our inactive and over quota policies.

Resolve common issues

Expand all zippies

Why didn’t my storage update after I deleted a lot of files?

After you delete a large number of files, it normally takes up to 48–72 hours for Google’s systems to accurately update the storage space.

If your storage fills again after successfully completing cleanup efforts, you might have files, such as photos and videos, set to back up automatically. When you delete a large number of files, unless automatic back up is turned off, files queued for back up are added to fill the free space. Learn more about Google Photos back ups.

I bought more storage through Google One, but it doesn’t show up. What should I do?

It can take up to 24 hours for a storage plan upgrade to become active. Try to sign out of your Google Account and sign in again. If it’s more than 24 hours, you can contact Google One support for help.

Do files in “Shared with me” use my storage space?

No, but they count against the file owner’s storage. However, if you make a copy of a shared file or add your own files into a folder someone shares with you, those new files use your storage.

Why does Drive for desktop on my computer show a different storage amount than the website?

Drive for desktop uses small placeholder files on your computer instead of downloading the full files from the cloud. Because they are only placeholders, these Drive for desktop files don’t reflect how much space you actually use in the cloud.

Your total cloud storage also counts hidden app data, background system files, and items left in your Drive Trash. These are not counted on Drive for desktop.

What are Google One storage clean up options for mobile?

You can easily clean up your storage on both Android and iOS with the storage manager.

What are Google One storage clean up options for web?

You can easily clean up your storage on the web with the storage manager.

How do I recover an accidentally deleted file?

  • Learn how to recover an accidentally deleted file in Google Drive.
  • Learn how to restore recently deleted photos and videos. I deleted thousands of old photos but my storage didn’t change. What happened?

Photos and videos backed up in “Storage Saver” formally “High quality,” before June 1, 2021, didn’t count against your storage. So, if you deleted “Storage Saver” photos backed up before this date, it doesn’t free up any space from your storage limit.

  • Set up recovery options for your Google Account & get sign-in help

  • On your computer, go to Google Drive, Docs, Sheets, Slides, or Vids.
  • Double-click or right-click the file you want to share.
  • Click Share .
  • Enter a recipient.
  • Click Send . For more details, see Get started with Slides.
Share your presentation with PowerPoint users
  • From a presentation, click FileEmail as attachment.
  • Under Attach as, choose the format (PowerPoint or PDF).
  • Enter the email address, subject, and message.
  • Click Send. For more details, see Work with Microsoft Office files.

Collaborate in real-time in SharePoint or OneDrive

Collaborate in real-time in Slides

When you share a presentation, depending on their access, collaborators can edit presentations, add comments, and assign tasks.

  • On your computer, open a document, spreadsheet, presentation, or video.
  • Highlight the text, images, cells, slides, or scenes you want to comment on.
  • To add a comment, in the toolbar, click Add comment .
  • Type your comment.
  • Click Comment. Access version history in SharePoint or OneDrive

Access version history in Slides

  • In Drive, open your file.
  • Click FileVersion historySee version history.
  • Click a timestamp to see a previous version of the file. Below the timestamp, you can review:
    • The names of people who edited the document.
    • The color next to each person’s name. The edits they made appear in that color.
  • (Optional) To revert to this version, click Restore this version. For more details, read See changes to your Drive files and folders.

Open a PowerPoint presentation

Open a PowerPoint presentation

  • In Drive, double-click a PowerPoint file.
  • A preview of your file opens.
  • At the top, click Open with Google Slides.
  • Any changes you make are saved to the original Microsoft Office file. For more details, see Work with Microsoft Office files.

Access a presentation offline in OneDrive

Access a presentation offline in Drive

  • Install the Google Docs Offline extension.
  • In Drive, click Settings Settings.
  • In the Offline section, check the Create, open and edit your recent Google Docs, Sheets, and Slides files on this device while offline box.
  • Click Done.
  • Right-click a file and turn on Available offline. To learn how to access files offline from your desktop or mobile, see Access stored Drive files without the internet.

Save a presentation automatically in SharePoint or OneDrive or turn on AutoRecover

Save a presentation automatically in Drive

Your presentation saves automatically in Drive as you work, so you don’t need to click Save.

Add images to your presentation

Add images to your presentation

You can drag and drop images from your computer into your presentation. Or, click InsertImage and choose an image from Google Drive, Google Photos, the web, and more.

For more details, see Add and edit images.

Add an Excel chart to your presentation

Add a Sheets chart to your presentation

  • On your computer, open a document or presentation in Google Docs or Google Slides.

  • Click Insert Chart From Sheets.

  • Click the spreadsheet with the chart you want to add, then click Select.

  • Click the chart you want to add.

    • If you don’t want the chart linked to the spreadsheet, uncheck “Link to spreadsheet.”

  • Click Import. For more details, see Insert and edit charts.

  • Note: The instructions in this guide are primarily web only.Get switching information for mobile devices.

Tool finder for Docs, Sheets, Slides & Vids

In Google Docs, Sheets, Slides, and Vids, you can use the tool finder at the top of the screen to:

  • Find menu items and tools.
  • View recommended actions. These can include recent actions you took in the document.
  • Get suggestions for related actions as you type.
  • Activate Find & replace after you enter content from your document.

Find tools & features

To find tools, in the toolbar at the top of the screen, select an option:

  • In the toolbar, click Search or in the menu, click Help Search the menus. To use a keyboard shortcut, press the following keys together:

  • PC: The Alt and / keys

  • Mac: The Option and / keys

  • ChromeOS: The Alt and / keys

Google Workspace Experiments

Create a Sheets canvas

Feature availability

  • This feature is available in English.
  • This feature requires an eligible Google Workspace or Google AI plan. Learn about Gemini features and plans.
  • It’s supported for Google Workspace Experiments, which is a trusted tester program for personal accounts to try new AI features. Learn more about Workspace Experiments.
  • If you have a personal Google Account, you might be able to use canvas and Build up to 3 times a month. Create interactive dashboards and apps in Google Sheets

Canvas may not be available if:

  • You’re using the Sheets app on your mobile device.

  • The combined data size of the source data tabs exceeds 1 million cells, which is not yet supported. Try reducing the number of rows, columns, or tabs.

  • The ability to download, copy, and/or print is disabled in Sheets.

  • You’re using a file stored in third-party storage or offline documents. You can only use canvas for files stored in Google Drive. To convert spreadsheet data into interactive visual layouts in Google Sheets, use canvas with Gemini. This will build custom interfaces to manage and update information. Examples include:

  • Dashboards

  • Heat maps

  • Kanban boards

  • Custom, interactive visualizations

Create a canvas

Important: For best feature performance, use the standard Google Sheets files. To use Gemini features in an Excel file, click File Save as Google Sheets.

To create a new canvas in Google Sheets:

  • On your computer, open a spreadsheet in Google Sheets.

  • Choose how you want to create your canvas. From the:

    • Side panel: Click Tools Create canvas .
    • Menu bar: Click Insert Create a canvas .
    • Bottom bar: Click Canvas menu Create a canvas.

  • In the prompt box, ask Gemini to build the interactive visualization. Example visualizations include:

    • Kanban boards:
      • Group tasks by status or category.
      • Drag and drop items to update progress in real time.
    • Gallery views: Display information in a card-based layout. You can sort and filter the data as needed.
    • Calendars: Plot campaign launch dates. Edit dates and create new events directly within the interface.
    • Dashboards: Perform data analysis on metrics, such as:
      • Pricing
      • Stock
      • Valuations

  • Optional: To make changes to your data after the canvas is generated, enter another prompt.

    • Example: “Convert this dashboard into dark mode.”

  • Optional: To share your canvas, on the top right, click Copy link . Access to canvas is the same as the spreadsheet access.

  • Optional: To ask Gemini to edit the canvas, on the top right, click and ask Gemini how you want to edit the canvas. Tips:

  • Canvases only use data from a single Sheet tab.

  • To find the underlying data on a canvas, click View data.

  • To find existing canvases, use the bottom bar menu.

Publish a Google Sheets canvas

You can publish a Google Sheets canvas with share settings separate from its underlying spreadsheet file.

  • Access control: View/Edit access to your spreadsheet file can remain restricted to a small team while your canvas is published to a larger group within your organization (or externally, depending on your workspace’s policy). External users with access to a published canvas cannot access your underlying spreadsheet file by default. However, they may see the data from that file as part of the canvas.
  • Target audience options:
    • Enterprise users: Target audience groups configured by your administrator appear in the access dropdown menu during publishing.
    • Consumer users: You can publish your canvas to the entire web. Note: The underlying data is sent directly to the browser, so viewers may be able to access it.

Manage your data in canvas

Important: The canvas provides a read-write interface for your spreadsheet data. If you have edit access, you can edit, delete, or add data directly within the canvas as an alternative to the grid view. If you have comment or view access, you can’t modify the canvas.

  • To change the name of your canvas, click the toolbar.
  • Enter a new name.
  • To save changes, click outside the toolbar.
    • Or, you can press Enter.
  • To manage your spreadsheet data, you can add, edit, or delete data directly in the canvas interface.
  • To remove the canvas, in the top toolbar, click Delete canvas .
    • If you don’t want to delete the canvas, you can keep it for future use.
  • To find the underlying data, click View data.
  • To exit the canvas, click any other tab in the spreadsheet.

Give feedback on generated suggestions

Google Workspace with Gemini is constantly learning and may not be able to support your request.

If you get a suggestion that’s inaccurate or that you feel is unsafe, you can let us know by submitting feedback. Your feedback can help improve AI-assisted Workspace features and broader Google efforts in AI.

Because feedback may be human readable, do not submit data that contains personal, confidential, or sensitive information.

  • To submit feedback, in any suggestion, click More Send feedback.
  • To provide general feedback on this feature, at the top, click Help Help Sheets improve.
  • To report a legal issue:
    • Create a request.
    • Click Send feedback Report an issue.
    • Click Suggest an idea Legal Help.

Learn about Workspace Experiments feature suggestions

  • Workspace Experiments feature suggestions don’t represent Google’s views, and should not be attributed to Google.
  • Don’t rely on Workspace Experiments features as medical, legal, financial or other professional advice.
  • Workspace Experiments features may suggest inaccurate or inappropriate information. Your feedback makes Workspace Experiments more helpful and safe.
  • Don’t include personal, confidential, or sensitive information in your prompts.
  • Google uses Workspace Experiments data and metrics to provide, improve, and develop products, services, and machine learning technologies across Google.
  • Your Workspace Experiments Data may also be read, rated, annotated, and reviewed by human reviewers. Importantly, where Google uses Google-selected input (as described in the Privacy Notice) to generate output, Google will aggregate and/or pseudonymize that content and resulting output before it is viewed by human reviewers, unless it is specifically provided as part of your feedback to Google.

Get started with Google Workspace Experiments

To get early access to AI features in Google Workspace, you can sign up for Google Workspace Experiments with your personal account. Workspace Experiments is a trusted tester program which allows you to try out new AI features. Features in Workspace Experiments are in development, and their quality and availability may vary.

Sign up for Workspace Experiments

What you need

  • Be 18 years or older.
  • A personal Google Account that you manage.
    • Workspace Experiments is not available for Google Workspace accounts. Tips:
  • You can get access to Workspace Experiments features in some countries and languages. Learn where you can use Workspace Experiments features.
  • To check if you have access to Workspace Experiments, look for the Gemini icon in a Workspace app like Gmail or Docs.
    • For example, at the top right of Gmail or Docs, click Ask Gemini . If it’s present, you have access to features in Workspace Experiments.

Learn about current Workspace Experiments features

As a Workspace Experiments participant, you get early access to generative AI features that change over time. Some features roll out gradually and might not be available to you yet.

Fix access issues with Workspace Experiments features

If you can’t access Workspace Experiments features, try the solutions below:

  • For English-only features, check that your language is set to English. Learn how to change the language for your Google Account.
  • Check if Workspace Experiments is available in your country. Learn more about where Workspace Experiments is available.
  • If you opt out of Workspace Experiments, you can’t access Workspace Experiments features.

Turn off Workspace Experiments features

To turn off Workspace Experiments features, you must exit Workspace Experiments. If you exit, you permanently lose access to all Workspace Experiments features, and you can’t rejoin Workspace Experiments.

To permanently exit Workspace Experiments, go to Google Docs, Google Slides, Sheets, Gmail, or Meet.

How to exit from Google Docs, Slides & Sheets

  • On your computer, open a file on Google Docs, Slides, or Sheets.

  • At the top, go to Tools Opt out of Workspace Experiments.

  • Click Confirm. How to exit from Gmail

  • On your computer, go to Gmail.

  • At the top, click Settings .

  • In the Workspace Experiments program notification, click Opt out.

  • Click Opt out.

  • Refresh your browser. How to exit from Meet

  • On your computer, go to Meet.

  • At the top right, click Settings .

  • On the left, click General.

  • Next to “You can use Workspace Experiments features,” click Opt out.

Insert shapes, images, and other objects

Crop & adjust images

You can crop, mask, and add borders to images you have added to Google Docs, Slides, and Vids.

Crop an image

You can trim the edges of an image or remove an unwanted section.

  • On your computer, open a document, presentation, or video.
  • Click on the image you want to crop.
  • Click Crop .
  • Around the border, click and drag the blue squares into the shape you want.
  • When you’re done, press Enter on your keyboard or click anywhere else in your file.

Make other adjustments to an image

You can also fit an image into a shape, add a border, adjust the color and filter, or reset an image.

Mask an image in Google Slides or Vids

To fit an image into a shape in Google Slides or Vids, you can mask an image.

  • On your computer, open a presentation or video.

  • Click the image you want to mask.

  • At the top, next to Crop , click the Down arrow .

  • Click the shape you want.

  • Your shape will mask your image. To adjust your shape, click and drag the colored handles. Add a border to an image

  • On your computer, open a document, presentation, or video.

  • Click the image you want to add a border to.

  • Click Border color .

  • Choose a color. Adjust color & filters

You can change the color, brightness, transparency, or contrast for one of your images.

  • On your computer, open a document, presentation, or video.
  • Click the image you want to adjust.
  • Click Format Format Options.
    • Under “Recolor,” change the color of your image.
    • Under “Adjustments,” change:
      • Opacity: How much of the slide shows behind the image.
      • Brightness: How bright the image appears on the slide.
      • Contrast: How much the image stands out from its background.
  • You’ll see your changes as you edit. Reset an image

If you want to undo the changes you’ve made to your photo, reset an image back to its original photo .

  • On your computer, open your document, presentation, or video.
  • Right-click an image.
  • Click Reset image .
  • The edited image will return to the original photo you added to the file.

Crop & adjust images (Computer)

You can crop, mask, and add borders to images you have added to Google Docs, Slides, and Vids.

Crop an image

You can trim the edges of an image or remove an unwanted section.

  • On your computer, open a document, presentation, or video.
  • Click on the image you want to crop.
  • Click Crop .
  • Around the border, click and drag the blue squares into the shape you want.
  • When you’re done, press Enter on your keyboard or click anywhere else in your file.

  • On your computer, open a presentation or video.

  • Click the image you want to mask.

  • At the top, next to Crop , click the Down arrow .

  • Click the shape you want.

  • Your shape will mask your image. To adjust your shape, click and drag the colored handles. Add a border to an image

    • Under “Recolor,” change the color of your image.

  • Click Reset image .
  • The edited image will return to the original photo you added to the file.

Crop & adjust images (iPhone and iPad)

You can crop, mask, and add borders to images you have added to Google Docs, Slides, and Vids.

To adjust image colors in Google Docs or Slides, go to docs.google.com or slides.google.com on a computer.

Important: On your mobile device, you can use a web browser to access Google Vids. You can watch processed videos, but you can’t edit videos on mobile.

Resize or rotate an image

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Docs app or Google Slides app.
  • Open a document or presentation.
    • In Google Docs, tap More turn on Print Layout.
  • Tap the image you want to adjust.
  • You can adjust the size of an image or rotate it:
    • Resize: Touch and drag the squares along the edges.
    • Rotate: Touch and drag the circle attached to the image.

Add a border to an image

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Docs app or Google Slides app.
  • Open a document or presentation.
  • Tap the image you want to add a border to.
  • Tap Format .
  • Choose a line color, weight, and dash.
Reset an image in Google Docs

If you want to undo the changes you made to your image:

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Docs app.
  • Open a document.
  • Tap the image you want to reset.
  • Tap Format .
  • Under “Image,” tap Reset image. To reset images in Google Slides, go to slides.google.com on a computer.
Crop or mask an image in Google Slides

To trim the edges of an image or fit an image into a shape, crop or mask the image.

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open the Google Slides app.
  • Open a presentation.
  • Tap the image you want to crop or mask.
  • Tap Crop .
    • To crop the image, touch and drag the border.
    • To mask the image, tap Mask tap the shape you want. The shape will cover your image. To adjust your shape, touch and drag the border. To crop an image in Google Docs, go to docs.google.com on a computer.

Insert and arrange text, shapes, diagrams, and lines

To enhance your presentation, you can add text, shapes, lines, and other objects in Google Slides.

Add text or objects to a slide

Insert a text box or object
  • On your computer, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Go to the slide where you want to add a text box or object.
  • At the top, click Insert.
  • Choose what you want to add, then click Text box, Image, Shape, or Line.
  • The object will get added to the slide and you can arrange or format it however you want. Tip: To edit a presentation on a touchscreen device, like a Pixel Book, double-tap the text box to start typing.

Remove a text box or object

  • On your computer, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Select the text box or object you want to remove.
  • At the top, click Edit.
  • Click Delete. Insert a diagram

Important: You can insert a diagram in any language that uses the Latin alphabet.

  • On your computer, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Go to the slide where you want to add the diagram.
  • At the top, click Insert Diagram.
  • Choose your style, number of levels, and template.
  • The diagram will get added to the slide. You can change it how you want.

Arrange and align objects on a slide

Arrange objects

  • On your computer, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Go to the slide where you want to arrange an object.
  • At the top, click Arrange.
  • Choose from the following options:
    • Order: Put the object behind or in front of text, other objects, or images.
    • Align: When you select multiple objects, you can align the edges of the objects.
    • Distribute: When you select three or more objects, you can evenly distribute the space between them.
    • Center on page: Center objects vertically or horizontally on a slide.
    • Rotate: Change the orientation of an object.
    • Group: Lock multiple objects together to make them easier to move around and format.

Align objects on a slide automatically

To easily align objects on a slide, click and drag any object where you want. There are two ways to align:

  • Snap to Guides: When you drag an object, you will see colored lines appear. These lines show what your object lines up with. This setting is turned on by default.
  • Snap to Grid: When you drag an object, it will align to an invisible grid on your slide. To turn on this setting, click View Snap to Grid. Show or hide rulers

You can show rulers on the canvas to help you place text or objects.

  • On your computer, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Click View Show ruler. Add or remove guide lines

You can add guide lines to the canvas to help you place text and objects.

  • On your computer, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Click View Guides. Choose an option.
  • Drag the line where you want. To remove a single line, right-click the line and click Delete guide.

To remove all guide lines, click View Guides Clear guides.

Move, rotate, or change the size of an object

  • On your computer, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Go to the slide with the object or text box you want to change.
  • Click the object or text box.
  • In the menu at the top, click Format Format options.
  • At the right, click Size & Position.
  • Make changes to the object or text box. Tip: To keep the height and width proportions when you resize an object, click Lock aspect ratio.

Insert and arrange text, shapes, diagrams, and lines (Computer)

Insert and arrange text, shapes, diagrams, and lines (iPhone and iPad)

To enhance your presentation, you can add text, shapes, lines, and other objects in Google Slides.

Add and remove text or objects

Add text boxes or objects

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open a presentation in the Google Slides app.

  • On the slide you want to add to, tap Add .

  • Choose what you want to add, then tap Text, Image, Shape, or Line. Remove text boxes or objects

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open a presentation in the Google Slides app.

  • Tap an object.

  • Tap Delete. If you don’t see “Delete,” you may have to tap the right arrow.

Arrange objects on a slide

Move an object

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open a presentation in the Google Slides app.

  • Tap the object you want to move.

  • Drag the object to a new location. Resize an object

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open a presentation in the Google Slides app.

  • Tap the object you want to resize.

  • Touch and drag the blue squares around the object. Rotate an object

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open a presentation in the Google Slides app.

  • Tap the object you want to rotate.

  • Touch and drag the blue circle at the top of the object. Change the order of objects

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open a presentation in the Google Slides app.

  • Tap the object you want to change the order of.

  • Tap what you want the object to do:

    • Move to front: Places the object in front of all other objects, so nothing overlaps it.
    • Send to back: Places the object behind other objects, which may overlap it. If you do not see “Move to front” or “Send to back,” you may have to tap the right arrow.

Select multiple objects

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open a presentation in the Google Slides app.
  • Touch and hold an object until the top bar turns blue.
  • To select other objects, tap them. To move more than one selected object, touch and drag.

Group objects together

If you want use multiple objects like a single object, you can group them together.

  • On your iPhone or iPad, open a presentation in the Google Slides app.
  • Touch and hold an object until the top bar turns blue.
  • Select the other objects you want to group together.
  • Tap Group . To separate the objects, tap Group again.

Present or print slides

Accept and present audience questions

You can start a live Q&A session in your Google Slides presentation and present questions at any time. Viewers can ask questions from any device.

Start a Q&A session

Accept audience questions
  • Open a Google Slides presentation.
  • At the top, next to Slideshow , click the Down arrow .
  • Click Presenter View.
  • In the new window, click Audience tools.
    • To start a new session, click Start new.
    • To resume a recent session, click Continue recent.
    • To end Q&A, click the on/off switch in the Q&A window.
      • Tip: Even if you don’t turn off Q&A, the Q&A view closes shortly after you end your Google Slide presentation. Tip: If you use Google through your work, school, or other organizations, you can choose who can submit questions:
  • In the “Presenter view” window, click Audience tools and change “Accepting questions from…”
Show audience questions

Presenters can display audience questions in a presentation:

  • Under “Audience Tools,” find a question to display.
  • Click Present.
    • To change the question, find a different question and click Present.
    • To hide the question, click Hide.
Review recent sessions

Presenters can see questions from recent Q&A sessions:

  • Open a Slides presentation.
  • At top, click Tools Q&A history.
  • Recent sessions appear on the right.
Ask a question

Audience members can ask questions in a presentation:

  • Go to the link at the top of the presentation.
  • Click Ask a question… and type a question.
    • Audience members can optionally check the box “Ask anonymously.”
  • Click Submit.
Vote on questions

Audience members can vote on the questions to be answered:

  • Go to the Q&A link shown on the slide.
  • Below the question you’d like to vote on, click Up vote or Down vote .

Accept and present audience questions (Computer)

You can start a live Q&A session in your Google Slides presentation and present questions at any time. Viewers can ask questions from any device.

  • In the “Presenter view” window, click Audience tools and change “Accepting questions from…”

Accept and present audience questions (iPhone and iPad)

You can start a live Q&A session in your Google Slides presentation and present questions at any time. Viewers can ask questions from any device.

Start a Q&A session

Accept audience questions
  • Present from your iPhone or iPad to another screen.
  • At the top right, tap Show Q&A :
    • To start a new session, tap Start new.
    • To resume a recent session, tap Continue recent.
    • To end Q&A, at the top right, tap the on/off switch.
      • Tip: Even if you don’t turn off Q&A, the Q&A view closes shortly after you end your Google Slide presentation.
Show audience questions

Presenters can display audience questions in a presentation:

  • At top right, tap Show Q&A .
  • Tap a question to display:
    • To change the question, tap a different question.
    • To hide the question, tap the check mark next to the question.
Ask a question

Audience members can ask questions during a presentation:

  • Go to the link at the top of the presentation.
  • Tap Ask a question… and type a question.
    • Audience members have the option to check the box “Ask anonymously.”
  • Tap Submit.
Vote on questions

Signed-in audience members can vote on questions to be answered:

  • Go to the link at the top of the presentation.
  • Below the question you’d like to vote on, tap Up vote or Down vote .

Present slides

Want advanced Google Workspace features for your business?

Try Google Workspace today!

You can present Google Slides so they show on a full screen.

Full-screen presentations work from a computer, mobile device, or TV with Chromecast or AirPlay. You can even accept audience questions while you present.

View a presentation

To present a full-screen presentation in Google Slides:

  • Open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • In the top right, click Slideshow . For best results, present with Google Chrome.
  • From the current slide, the presentation will become full screen. To change slides, use the arrow keys on your keyboard or click the arrows at the bottom of the presentation.
  • To exit full-screen, press the Esc key.

Present with notes, auto-advance slides & more

View a presentation with speaker notes
  • Open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • In the top right corner, next to Slideshow , click the Down arrow .
  • Click Presenter view.
  • Click Speaker notes.
Advance slides automatically

You can set your slides to play automatically when:

  • You present your presentation.
  • Someone clicks on a link to your published presentation.
  • Someone views your published presentation that’s embedded in a website.

Advance slides automatically while you present

  • Open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • In the top right corner, click Slideshow .
  • At the bottom, click Options Auto advance options.
  • Choose how quickly to advance your slides. Tip: Re-set the auto advance options each time you present your presentation.

Advance slides automatically for a published presentation

Important:

  • If you use Google Slides through work or school, you can only share the link with people within your organization.
  • After you publish your presentation, anyone with the link will be able to see your presentation.
  • Open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Click File Publish to the web.
  • Choose Link or Embed.
  • Under “Auto-advance slides,” choose how much time you want to add between slides.
  • Click Publish Ok. Tip: If you want to change your presentation or timing, you’ll need to copy a new link.
Stop publishing
  • Open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Click File Publish to the web.
  • At the bottom, click Published content & settings Stop publishing.
Present with other monitors

Important:

  • To present with other monitors, you must use a computer with a Google Chrome browser and have at least one extra monitor attached.
  • Presentation display options are cleared when the presentation is reloaded.
  • On your computer, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • At the top right corner, next to “Slideshow,” click the dropdown arrow .
  • Select Presentation display options .
    • If you have yet to give Google Chrome permissions to use the multi-display support, select Allow Allow. Learn how to change site settings permissions.
    • If you deny Google Chrome permissions, click Learn more.
  • You can select from “Presenter view,” “Present from beginning,” and “Full screen.”
  • Select which monitor you want to display your presentation to.
    • You can’t use the “Presenter view” and “Full screen” at the same time with one monitor.
    • When you deselect “Full screen,” you can only present to your current monitor.
  • To begin your presentation, select Start slideshow.
Highlight & annotate as you present

When you present, you can use a pen tool to draw or make annotations. Annotations stay during your presentation, but disappear after you end your slideshow.

Use the pen tool during a slideshow
  • On your browser, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • At the top right corner, click Slideshow .
  • At the bottom left, click Options Enable pen tool.
  • To draw or annotate, click and drag on your slide.
  • Optional:
    • To change the pen color, at the bottom, click Pen tool Select a color.
    • To erase annotations on the current slide, at the bottom, click Erase Erase all.
    • To turn off the pen tool during your presentation, choose an option:
      • At the bottom, click Pen tool Turn off.
      • At the bottom, click Options Disable pen tool.

Other actions while presenting

When you present, you can choose more options from the toolbar at the bottom of the presentation window:

  • Select slides to present from a list
  • Open “Presenter” view
  • Turn on laser pointer
  • Print the presentation
  • Download the presentation in PDF or PPTX format Present mode keyboard shortcuts

Present slides (Computer)

Want advanced Google Workspace features for your business?

Try Google Workspace today!

You can present Google Slides so they show on a full screen.

View a presentation

To present a full-screen presentation in Google Slides:

  • Open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • In the top right, click Slideshow . For best results, present with Google Chrome.
  • From the current slide, the presentation will become full screen. To change slides, use the arrow keys on your keyboard or click the arrows at the bottom of the presentation.
  • To exit full-screen, press the Esc key.

Present with notes, auto-advance slides & more

View a presentation with speaker notes
  • Open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • In the top right corner, next to Slideshow , click the Down arrow .
  • Click Presenter view.
  • Click Speaker notes.
Advance slides automatically

You can set your slides to play automatically when:

  • You present your presentation.
  • Someone clicks on a link to your published presentation.
  • Someone views your published presentation that’s embedded in a website.

Advance slides automatically while you present

  • Open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • In the top right corner, click Slideshow .
  • At the bottom, click Options Auto advance options.
  • Choose how quickly to advance your slides. Tip: Re-set the auto advance options each time you present your presentation.

Advance slides automatically for a published presentation

Important:

  • If you use Google Slides through work or school, you can only share the link with people within your organization.
  • After you publish your presentation, anyone with the link will be able to see your presentation.
  • Open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Click File Publish to the web.
  • Choose Link or Embed.
  • Under “Auto-advance slides,” choose how much time you want to add between slides.
  • Click Publish Ok. Tip: If you want to change your presentation or timing, you’ll need to copy a new link.
Stop publishing
  • Open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • Click File Publish to the web.
  • At the bottom, click Published content & settings Stop publishing.
Present with other monitors

Important:

  • To present with other monitors, you must use a computer with a Google Chrome browser and have at least one extra monitor attached.
  • Presentation display options are cleared when the presentation is reloaded.
  • On your computer, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • At the top right corner, next to “Slideshow,” click the dropdown arrow .
  • Select Presentation display options .
    • If you have yet to give Google Chrome permissions to use the multi-display support, select Allow Allow. Learn how to change site settings permissions.
    • If you deny Google Chrome permissions, click Learn more.
  • You can select from “Presenter view,” “Present from beginning,” and “Full screen.”
  • Select which monitor you want to display your presentation to.
    • You can’t use the “Presenter view” and “Full screen” at the same time with one monitor.
    • When you deselect “Full screen,” you can only present to your current monitor.
  • To begin your presentation, select Start slideshow.
Highlight & annotate as you present

When you present, you can use a pen tool to draw or make annotations. Annotations stay during your presentation, but disappear after you end your slideshow.

Use the pen tool during a slideshow
  • On your browser, open a presentation in Google Slides.
  • At the top right corner, click Slideshow .
  • At the bottom left, click Options Enable pen tool.
  • To draw or annotate, click and drag on your slide.
  • Optional:
    • To change the pen color, at the bottom, click Pen tool Select a color.
    • To erase annotations on the current slide, at the bottom, click Erase Erase all.
    • To turn off the pen tool during your presentation, choose an option:
      • At the bottom, click Pen tool Turn off.
      • At the bottom, click Options Disable pen tool.

Other actions while presenting

When you present, you can choose more options from the toolbar at the bottom of the presentation window:

  • Select slides to present from a list
  • Open “Presenter” view
  • Turn on laser pointer
  • Print the presentation
  • Download the presentation in PDF or PPTX format Present mode keyboard shortcuts
